```
     1→## Data Value Contract
     2→
     3→- Never persist, publish, or return `null` for domain data values (Surreal records, API payloads, events, UI state).
     4→- Absence must be represented by omitted optional fields only (`field?: Type`), not by `null`.
     5→- If `null` appears in domain data, treat it as a contract violation and fix the producer. Do NOT sanitize/coerce it at consumers.
     6→
     7→## TypeScript Conventions
     8→
     9→- Do NOT use `null`. Use `undefined` via optional properties (`field?: Type`) instead.
    10→- Do NOT create wrapper/helper functions for simple operations. Cast directly with `as`.
    11→- Type result payloads once and avoid repetitive per-field casting.
    12→
    13→
    14→
    15→## Failure Handling
    16→
    17→- Do NOT add fallback logic that masks invalid state, malformed payloads, or contract violations.
    18→- Fail fast: throw immediately when required data is missing or does not match the expected shape.
    19→- Prefer explicit hard failures over silent degradation, synthetic defaults, or "best effort" recovery.
    20→- Only introduce fallback behavior when explicitly requested, and document the reason in code comments.
    21→
    22→## Testing Setup
    23→
    24→- Install deps: `bun install`
    25→- Run deterministic unit tests (no LLM/API calls): `bun test tests/unit/`
    26→- Run smoke test: `bun test tests/smoke/`
    27→- Run eval suite: `bun run eval`
    28→- Run eval watch mode: `bun run eval:watch`
    29→- Agents must not run evals directly. Delegate eval execution to the user and ask them to run eval commands and share results.
    30→
    31→### Smoke Test Isolation
    32→
    33→- Smoke tests create an isolated Surreal namespace/database, apply `schema/surreal-schema.surql`, run assertions, then remove the test DB/namespace.
    34→- Smoke tests require a reachable SurrealDB server at `SURREAL_URL` with credentials from env.
    35→- Smoke tests boot a dedicated app server process with test `SURREAL_NAMESPACE`, `SURREAL_DATABASE`, and `PORT`; do not point smoke runs at shared production-like DBs.
    36→
    37→### Eval Requirements
    38→
    39→- Evals call the real extraction model through existing app wiring.
    40→- Required env: `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` and `EXTRACTION_MODEL` (set to Haiku model when needed).
    41→- Optional env:
    42→  - `AUTOEVAL_MODEL` for `autoevals` factuality scorer model override.
    43→  - `EVAL_RESULTS_DIR` for evalite sqlite output.
    44→  - `EVAL_CACHE_DIR` for extraction eval cache.
    45→
    46→## Server Architecture Overview
    47→
    48→- Entrypoint is `app/server.ts`; it only calls `startServer()` from `app/src/server/runtime/start-server.ts`.
    49→- Runtime bootstrap is split into:
    50→  - `runtime/config.ts` (env parsing/validation)
    51→  - `runtime/dependencies.ts` (Surreal + model clients)
    52→  - `runtime/start-server.ts` (route registration + Bun server startup)
    53→- HTTP cross-cutting concerns live in `app/src/server/http`:
    54→  - `request-logging.ts` (request context + top-level error boundary)
    55→  - `response.ts` (JSON/headers helpers)
    56→  - `parsing.ts` (request/form-data parsing)
    57→  - `errors.ts` + `observability.ts` (error/log primitives)
    58→- SSE state management is isolated in `app/src/server/streaming/sse-registry.ts`.
    59→- Route/business domains are separated by workflow:
    60→  - `workspace/*` for workspace create/bootstrap/scope checks
    61→  - `chat/*` for ingress + async message processing
    62→  - `entities/*` for entity search endpoints
    63→  - `onboarding/*` for onboarding state and guided replies
    64→  - `extraction/*` for extraction generation, persistence, dedupe/upsert, embeddings, and context loaders
    65→- `graph/*` contains reusable Surreal graph queries used by chat/tools and higher-level workflows.
    66→
    67→### Primary Chat Flow (`POST /api/chat/messages`)
    68→
    69→- `chat/chat-ingress.ts` validates/persists user input and registers an SSE stream message id.
    70→- `chat/chat-processor.ts` orchestrates async processing:
    71→  - load conversation + graph context
    72→  - run extraction (message and optional attachment chunks)
    73→  - persist entities/relationships/provenance
    74→  - transition onboarding state
    75→  - generate assistant response (onboarding reply or graph-aware chat)
    76→  - emit SSE events (`token`, `extraction`, `onboarding_seed`, `onboarding_state`, `assistant_message`, `done|error`)
    77→
    78→### RecordId and Table Access Rules
    79→
    80→- Use `RecordId` objects everywhere for Surreal identifiers (never `table:id` strings).
    81→- Server extraction types define typed record aliases:
    82→  - `GraphEntityRecord` and `SourceRecord` include a typed `tb` field for table discrimination.
    83→- Prefer typed `record.tb` access for table branching; do not use untyped casts like `(record as unknown as { tb: string }).tb`.
    84→
    85→## SurrealDB SDK v2
    86→
    87→Reference: https://surrealdb.com/learn/fundamentals/schemafull/define-fields
    88→
    89→- When using `http://` or `https://` URLs, the SDK uses HTTP transport only. It does NOT attempt WebSocket upgrade.
    90→- WebSocket is only used when the URL scheme is `ws://` or `wss://`.
    91→- To CREATE a record with a specific ID, use the SDK's `RecordId` class:
    92→  ```typescript
    93→  import { RecordId } from "surrealdb";
    94→  const record = new RecordId("table", "my-id");
    95→  await query("CREATE $record CONTENT $content;", { record, content: {...} });
    96→  ```
    97→- To SELECT a specific record by ID, use `RecordId` in the FROM clause:
    98→  ```typescript
    99→  const record = new RecordId("gladiator", id);
   100→  await query("SELECT * FROM $record;", { record });
   101→  ```
   102→- Do NOT use string-based record IDs like `table:id` in queries - use `RecordId` objects.
   103→- For optional record fields (e.g. `option<record<match>>`), omit the field instead of setting `null` - SurrealDB defaults to `NONE`.
   104→- Query results return `RecordId` objects for record references - cast directly, no string conversion needed.
   105→- Clause order: `SELECT ... FROM ... WHERE ... LIMIT ... FETCH ...` - `LIMIT` must come before `FETCH`.
   106→- ORDER BY fields must be in the SELECT clause - you cannot order by fields not selected.
   107→- Always include every `ORDER BY` field in the `SELECT` projection. Example: if ordering decisions/questions by `created_at DESC`, select `summary, created_at` or `text, created_at` (not just `summary` / `text`).
   108→- Known issue: Surreal can throw `Expected a single result output when using the ONLY keyword` when a statement uses `ONLY` and returns no record output.
   109→- Workaround: force a return clause. In SDK calls that map to `ONLY` (for example `relate(...)`), use `.output("after")` so the statement returns a record.
   110→- Type query results directly. Access `RecordId.id` directly - do NOT create wrapper functions like `extractRecordId()`, `toString()`, etc:
   111→  ```typescript
   112→  const rows = await selectMany("SELECT id, name FROM gladiator WHERE totalWins > 0;") as Array<{ id: RecordId; name: string }>;
   113→  const result = rows.map((row) => ({ gladiatorId: row.id.id as string, name: row.name }));
   114→  ```
   115→- Do NOT use `FETCH` in queries if you need the raw RecordId reference. `FETCH` resolves references to full objects.
   116→- For `TYPE RELATION` tables, do NOT write edges with `CREATE`. `CREATE` produces non-relation records and will fail relation constraints. Use `RELATE` (or SDK `relate(...)`) to create actual relation edges.
   117→- For nested arrays (2D grids), use explicit type: `DEFINE FIELD grid ON match TYPE array<array<string>>;` - plain `array` silently rejects nested arrays in SCHEMAFULL mode.
   118→- Keep all Surreal tables in `schema/surreal-schema.surql` as `SCHEMAFULL`. Do NOT introduce `SCHEMALESS` tables.
   119→- In `SCHEMAFULL`, every persisted nested key must be explicitly declared. For `array<object>` fields, always define `field[*].subField` entries for all written properties.
   120→- Do NOT rely on permissive object inference for production payloads. If code writes a new key, update schema in the same change before deploy.
   121→- After schema changes, verify with `INFO FOR TABLE <table>;` in the target namespace/database to confirm nested fields are present.
   122→

   138→## SurrealQL Essentials

   142→- **CREATE:** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/statements/create
   143→- **UPDATE:** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/statements/update

   149→

   151→- **DEFINE FIELD (types, defaults, assertions):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/statements/define/field
   152→- **DEFINE INDEX (unique, full-text, vector):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/statements/define/indexes
   153→- **DEFINE EVENT (triggers on record changes):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/statements/define/event

   156→## Graph Relationships (Critical for our data model)
   157→
   158→- **Graph Model Overview (best practices, when to use edges vs record links):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealdb/models/graph
   159→- **RELATE Statement (create edges):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/statements/relate
   160→- **Graph Relations Fundamentals (RELATE, INSERT RELATION, arrow syntax):** https://surrealdb.com/learn/fundamentals/relationships/graph-relations
   161→- **Three Ways to Model Relationships (record links vs references vs graph edges):** https://surrealdb.com/blog/three-ways-to-model-data-relationships-in-surrealdb
   162→- **Graph Traversal, Recursion & Shortest Path:** https://surrealdb.com/blog/data-analysis-using-graph-traversal-recursion-and-shortest-path
   163→
   164→### Key SurrealQL graph syntax:
   165→```sql
   166→-- Create edge
   167→RELATE person:marcus->owns->project:brain;
   168→
   169→-- Traverse forward
   170→SELECT ->owns->project FROM person:marcus;
   171→
   172→-- Traverse backward
   173→SELECT <-owns<-person FROM project:brain;
   174→
   175→-- Multi-hop
   176→SELECT ->has_feature->feature->has_task->task FROM project:brain;
   177→
   178→-- Bidirectional
   179→SELECT <->conflicts_with<->decision FROM decision:d1;
   180→
   181→-- Edge with metadata
   182→RELATE decision:d1->conflicts_with->decision:d2
   183→  SET severity = 'hard', description = 'Contradictory deadlines';
   184→
   185→-- Recursive traversal (org tree, dependency chains)
   186→record:root.{..}.{ id, ->depends_on->task.@ };
   187→
   188→-- TYPE RELATION enforces edge-only tables
   189→DEFINE TABLE owns TYPE RELATION IN person OUT project | feature | task;
   190→```
   191→
   192→## Vector Search (For semantic search + RAG context)
   193→
   194→- **Vector Search Reference Guide:** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealdb/reference-guide/vector-search
   195→- **DEFINE INDEX (HNSW & MTREE):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/statements/define/indexes
   196→- **OpenAI Embeddings Integration:** https://surrealdb.com/docs/integrations/embeddings/openai
   197→- **Mistral Embeddings Integration:** https://surrealdb.com/docs/integrations/embeddings/mistral
   198→- **Python Embeddings (patterns transferable to JS):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/integrations/embeddings/python
   199→- **Full-Text to Vector Search Migration Guide:** https://surrealdb.com/blog/moving-from-full-text-search-to-vector-search-in-surrealdb
   200→- **Hybrid Search (vector + full-text with RRF):** https://surrealdb.com/blog/hybrid-vector-text-search-in-the-terminal-with-surrealdb-and-ratatui
   201→- **Search Functions (search::score, search::rrf):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/functions/database/search
   202→- **Vector Functions (distance, similarity):** https://surrealdb.com/docs/surrealql/functions/database/vector
   203→
   204→### Key SurrealQL vector syntax:
   205→```sql
   206→-- Define embedding field + HNSW index
   207→DEFINE FIELD embedding ON conversation TYPE array<float>;
   208→DEFINE INDEX idx_conv_embedding ON conversation FIELDS embedding
   209→  HNSW DIMENSION 1536 DIST COSINE;
   210→
   211→-- KNN search (top 5 nearest neighbors)
   212→SELECT *, vector::similarity::cosine(embedding, $query_vec) AS similarity
   213→FROM conversation
   214→WHERE embedding <|5, COSINE|> $query_vec
   215→ORDER BY similarity DESC;
   216→
   217→-- Hybrid search (vector + full-text via RRF)
   218→LET $vs = SELECT id FROM conversation WHERE embedding <|5, COSINE|> $query_vec;
   219→LET $ft = SELECT id, search::score(1) AS score FROM conversation WHERE text @1@ 'decision about auth';
   220→RETURN search::rrf([$vs, $ft], 5);
   221→```
   222→

   255→
   256→## Platform-Specific Patterns
   257→
   258→### Pattern: Extraction Pipeline Write
   259→After the LLM extracts entities from a message, write them to the graph in a single transaction:
   260→```sql
   261→BEGIN TRANSACTION;
   262→  -- Create entities
   263→  LET $task = CREATE task SET title = $title, status = 'open', owner = $owner;
   264→  LET $decision = CREATE decision SET summary = $summary, status = 'extracted', confidence = $conf;
   265→  
   266→  -- Create relationships
   267→  RELATE $decision->decided_in->$conversation;
   268→  RELATE $decision->belongs_to->$project;
   269→  RELATE $task->belongs_to->$feature;
   270→  RELATE $person->owns->$task;
   271→COMMIT TRANSACTION;
   272→```
   273→
   274→### Pattern: Cross-Project Conflict Detection
   275→Traverse the graph to find related entities across projects:
   276→```sql
   277→-- Find all decisions in projects that share dependencies
   278→SELECT 
   279→  id, summary, status,
   280→  <-belongs_to<-project AS projects,
   281→  ->conflicts_with->decision AS conflicts
   282→FROM decision
   283→WHERE status IN ['extracted', 'proposed', 'confirmed']
   284→  AND <-belongs_to<-project != $current_project;
   285→```
   286→
   287→### Pattern: Context Packet for MCP Server
   288→Build a token-budgeted context packet for coding agents:
   289→```sql
   290→-- Active decisions for a project
   291→SELECT summary, rationale, status, decided_at
   292→FROM decision
   293→WHERE <-belongs_to<-project = $project
   294→  AND status IN ['confirmed', 'proposed']
   295→ORDER BY decided_at DESC
   296→LIMIT 20;
   297→
   298→-- Dependency chain for current task
   299→SELECT 
   300→  id, title, status,
   301→  ->depends_on->task.{id, title, status} AS dependencies
   302→FROM task
   303→WHERE id = $current_task;
   304→```
   305→
```
